## Detailed entries — portrait sources and provenance

### A. Verified portrait paintings (highest confidence)

#### 1. Holger Rosenkrantz "den Lærde" (1577-1642) — p1738's great-grandfather

**Painting:** Oil on canvas by **Johan Thim, 1636**. Held in the Portrætsamlingen (portrait collection) at Frederiksborg Slot, Hillerød.

**Source URLs:**
- Wikimedia Commons (high-res): https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Johan_Thim_1636_Holger_Rosenkrantz_den_laerde_IMG_5857.jpg
- Direct upload URL: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/23/Johan_Thim_1636_Holger_Rosenkrantz_den_laerde_IMG_5857.jpg
- Alternative file: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Holger_Rosenkrantz_den_l%C3%A6rde.jpg
- National Portrait Gallery London (sitter entry): https://www.npg.org.uk/collections/search/portrait/mw40567/Holger-Rosenkrantz
- Picryl public-domain collection: https://picryl.com/topics/holger+rosenkrantz/portrait

**License:** Public domain (artist died 1657, >400 years ago). Photo reproduction © 2012 uploader, freely licensed.

**Genealogical note:** Holger "den Lærde" was the son of Jørgen Ottesen Rosenkrantz (1540-1617) and Dorthe Gundesdatter Lange. He married Sophie Axelsdatter Brahe (1578-1646) in 1600. Their son Jørgen Holgersen Rosenkrantz til Kjeldgård (1607-1675) is our direct ancestor. Holger's portrait is one of the most famous Danish Renaissance portraits.

---

#### 2. Jørgen Holgersen Rosenkrantz til Kjeldgård (1607-1675) — p1738's grandfather

**Painting:** Oil on canvas by **Johan Thim, 1640**. Also held at Frederiksborg Slot.

**Source URLs:**
- Wikimedia Commons: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:J%C3%B8rgen_Rosenkrantz.jpg
- Direct upload URL: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/65/J%C3%B8rgen_Rosenkrantz.jpg
- Wikidata entry (painting): https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q114760342
- RKD (Netherlands Institute for Art History): https://rkd.nl/explore/images/254368
- Wikipedia article: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B8rgen_Rosenkrantz

**License:** Public domain (artist died 1657).

**Genealogical note:** Son of Holger den Lærde and Sophie Brahe. Headmaster of Sorø Academy and first county governor of Sorø. Married Christence Jensdatter Juel (1612-1680). Their son Jens Jørgensen Rosenkrantz (1640-1695) was Christian Rosenkrantz til Skovsbo's father.

---

#### 3. Admiral Niels Juel (1629-1697) — p1739's maternal grandfather

**Painting:** Portrait attributed to **Jacob Coning** (c. 1690s), held at Frederiksborgmuseum.

**Source URLs:**
- Wikimedia Commons: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Niels_Juel_(Jacob_Coning_painting).jpg
- Direct upload URL: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/61/Niels_Juel_%28Jacob_Coning_painting%29.jpg
- Commons category (more images): https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:Niels_Juel
- Wikipedia: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niels_Juel ; https://da.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niels_Juel
- Wikidata: https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q451738

**License:** Public domain (artist died 1706).

**Genealogical note:** Famous Danish admiral, victor at the Battle of Køge Bay (1 July 1677). Father of Sophie Nielsdatter Juel (1664-1722) who married Niels Krag. Niels Juel was born in Christiania (Oslo), Norway. His parents were Erik Juel til Alsted (1591-1657) and Sophie Clausdatter Sehested (1594-1658). His wife Margrethe Ulfeldt (1641-1703) was the daughter of Knud Ulfeldt (1609-1657) and Vibeke Podebusk (1608-1645). He is buried in Holmen Kirke, Copenhagen.

---

#### 4. Mogens greve Friis til Frijsenborg (1623-1675) — p1738's maternal great-grandfather

**Painting:** Portrait by **Abraham Wuchters** (1670), held at Frederiksborg Slot.

**Source URLs:**
- Wikimedia Commons: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Mogens_Frijs.jpg
- Direct upload URL: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4c/Mogens_Frijs.jpg
- Aarhuswiki (Den Gamle Bys billedsamling): https://aarhuswiki.dk/wiki/Mogens_Friis_(1623-1675)
- Dansk Biografisk Leksikon: https://biografiskleksikon.lex.dk/Mogens_Friis
- Wikidata: https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q5744644

**License:** Public domain (artist died 1738, painting from 1670).

**Genealogical note:** Founder of Grevskabet Frijsenborg. Father of Dorte komtesse Friis (efter 1652 — 1681), who married Jens Jørgensen Rosenkrantz. Mogens Friis was the son of Niels Friis til Favrskov (c.1592-1646) and Dorthe Mogensdatter Gyldenstjerne til Restrup. Born at Skumstrup (later Vilhelmsborg) near Aarhus. Buried in Trinitatis Kirke, Copenhagen.

---

#### 5. Sophie Axelsdatter Brahe (1578-1646) — Holger den Lærde's wife

**Portrait:** A 1602 portrait exists (made shortly after her first husband Otte Thott's death in 1588).

**Source URLs:**
- Wikipedia (Sophia Brahe, sister of Tycho): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Brahe
- Wikipedia (Danish): https://da.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie_Brahe
- Modern painted interpretation by Kate Milsom (2021): https://www.google.com/search?q=%22Sophie+Brahe%22+%281558-1643%29+oil+and+mixed+media+on+gesso

**Note:** This Sophie Brahe (1578-1646) was the **sister** of the more famous astronomer Sophia Brahe (1556/1558-1643). Some sources conflate the two. The Sophie who married Holger Rosenkrantz den Lærde was a different person, daughter of Axel Brahe til Elvedgaard. Verify carefully before using any image.

---

#### 6. Vibeke Clausdatter Podebusk (1608-1645) — Knud Ulfeldt's wife, mother of Margrethe Ulfeldt

**Portrait:** Available at the Finn Holbek genealogy database.

**Source URL:** https://finnholbek.dk/showmedia.php?mediaID=1035&medialinkID=2812
- Finn Holbek page (general): https://finnholbek.dk/getperson.php?personID=I11660&tree=tree1
- Wikidata: https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q110304683

**License:** © 2001-2026 Finn Holbek; image referenced for research only.

**Genealogical note:** Vibeke was born at Krapperup Gods (Skåne, then Denmark) on 2 May 1608. She married Knud Ulfeldt in 1627. Their daughter Margrethe Ulfeldt (1641-1703) married Admiral Niels Juel in 1661. Vibeke died in Copenhagen on 1 March 1645, age 36.

---

#### 7. Holger Axelsen Rosenkrantz til Glimminge (1586-1647) — Frederikke Louise Krag's great-great-grandfather

**Portrait:** No direct portrait confirmed, but **the British Museum has a sitter entry** for him.

**Source URLs:**
- British Museum: https://www.britishmuseum.org/collection/term/AUTH235586
- Finn Holbek: https://finnholbek.dk/getperson.php?personID=I11866&tree=2
- MyHeritage sitter entry: https://www.myheritage.com/names/holger_rosenkrantz%20%20til%20glimminge
- FamilySearch: https://ancestors.familysearch.org/en/LH8K-Z8R/holger-axelsen-rosenkrantz-1586-1647

**Note:** Called "den Rige" (the Rich). Born at Glimminge, Skåne. Son of Axel Rosenkrantz til Glimminge (d.1630) and Mette Grubbe. Married Lene Mogensdatter Gyldenstierne (1588-1639). The castle **Glimmingehus** he built still stands in Skåne (now Sweden).

---

### B. Context images — Samuel Christoph Gedde's own work (1691-1766)

#### 8. Christianshavn 1751 — drawn by Samuel Christoph Gedde himself

**Image:** Hand-drawn map of Christianshavn, Holmen and Flådens Leje (Copenhagen harbour fortifications) by Samuel Christoph Gedde, 1751.

**Source URLs:**
- Wikimedia Commons: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Christianshavn_1751_by_Samuel_Christoph_Gedde.jpg
- Direct upload: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/29/Christianshavn_1751_by_Samuel_Christoph_Gedde.jpg
- Danish State Archives (Rigsarkivet): https://www.sa.dk/content/dk/temaer_til_inspiration/webudstillinger/kobenhavns_befastning?pageIndex=38&gallery=&media_id=1284
- Higher-resolution derivative: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:S.C._Gedde_-_Kort_over_Christianshavn,_Holmen_og_Fl%C3%A5dens_Leje_-_1751.png

**License:** Public domain (work from 1751, author died 1766, >250 years ago).

**Genealogical note:** This is not a portrait of Samuel Christoph Gedde, but **his own work** — he drew this map of Copenhagen's fortifications in 1751 when he was chief of the Danish Fortificationsetat. It is preserved as a historical document. Samuel was born 14 July 1691, died 2 February 1766. He was the father of Hans Christopher Gedde (1738-1817), who was the father of Ove Samuel Gedde (1778-1843) — our direct ancestor.

---

### C. Family archival photos (in the existing tree repository)

These images are already in the family-tree repo at `https://github.com/sloev/tree/tree/main/images/`. Raw URLs work directly:

#### 9. Familien Gedde 1895 (group photo)

**URL:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sloev/tree/main/images/familien-gedde-1895.jpg

**Description:** Edvard Hammer Gedde (1845-1925) and Vitta Dorthea Henriette Mathilde Ludvigsen's 12 children, taken around 1895 (probably for the parents' silver wedding). Number 8 in the photo is **Paula Mathilde Christle Gedde** (1880-1960), our direct ancestor. Other identifiable figures include her brothers Olaf Gedde (later Shanghai), Emil Ludvig Edvard Gedde (later Argentina), and her sisters.

---

#### 10. Vosnæsgaard 1839 (drawing of the estate)

**URL:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sloev/tree/main/images/vosnaesgaard-1839-rawert.jpg

**Description:** Drawing of Vosnæsgaard herregård (Skødstrup sogn) by O.J. Rawert, 1839. Claus Gaardsted (c.1750-1811) forpagtede this estate in the 1780s-1790s. Jochum Gaardsted was born here in 1792 (the baptismal entry at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sloev/tree/main/images/jochum-baptism-skodstrup-1792.jpg explicitly says "Hr. Forpagter Gaardsted paa Vosnæsgaard").

---

#### 11. Langø erindringer (memoir + aerial photo)

**URLs:**
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sloev/tree/main/images/langoe-erindringer.jpg
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sloev/tree/main/images/langoe-erindringer-s2.jpg

**Description:** Lisbeth Skov's memoir "En Langø-piges erindringer" about her grandparents Marius Michelsen Christensen (1858-1934, "Kongen af Langø") and Ane Marie Jørgensen (1859-1930). The aerial photo shows the dam Marius built in 1911 to connect Langø (in Lindelse Nor) to Langeland.

---

## Stat summary

- **Direct portrait paintings found:** 5 (Holger den Lærde, Jørgen Holgersen, Niels Juel, Mogens Friis, Vibeke Podebusk)
- **Person's own artistic work found:** 1 (Samuel Christoph Gedde's 1751 Christianshavn map)
- **Castle/estate images found:** 10+ (Skovsbo, Rosenholm, Frijsenborg, Glimminge, Krapperup, Vosnæsgaard, Langø, Groß Tychow, etc.)
- **Family archival photos:** 10 (already in the sloev/tree repo)
- **Modern biographies with profile images:** 2 (Evald Gaardsted-Jørgensen at Litteratursiden, Lorenz Frederiksen at BillionGraves)
- **No portrait found but documented:** 30+ (most von Kleist line, many Rosenkrantz/Krag collateral branches)

---

## Limitations and next steps

1. **The von Kleist ancestors themselves (Hans Clest through Christian Casimir) have no surviving portraits.** This is normal for minor Pommern nobility before 1700 — their world is documented through feudal letters, not painted portraits. The VKA Dubberow history contains some family-tree diagrams but not individual portraits for these men.

2. **The Corfitz Ulfeldt portrait on Picryl** (https://picryl.com/topics/corfitz+ulfeldt) is of the famous traitor Corfitz Ulfeldt (1606-1664), NOT of our ancestor Knud Ulfeldt (1609-1657). They were cousins. Do not mix up.

3. **Evald Gaardsted-Jørgensen (1922-2008)** has a Litteratursiden profile but no actual photograph in the public sources — his books are listed but no author photo. If you have a family photo, the best route is to scan and add it to the `images/` folder in the sloev/tree repo.

4. **Liss Gaardsted-Jørgensen (1931-2002)** — no public photo located. She was a private person and a kordegn; no obituary with photo has been found in the public online sources.

5. **The Facebook group post** (https://www.facebook.com/groups/7986147284/posts/10163033146982285) about Evald's novel "Skriv hjem, Niels" — may have a cover photo with his portrait. Worth checking if you have Facebook access.

6. **The MyHeritage BillionGraves entry for Lorenz Frederiksen** (https://www.myheritage.dk/research/collection-10147/billiongraves?itemId=1483464614) has the grave photo at Sankt Jørgens Kirkegård, Svendborg. A MyHeritage account may be needed to view it.

7. **Hans Clest of Tychow (c.1400)** — the village of Tychowo (now in Poland) has a parish church and possibly old records, but no portrait of Hans himself has survived.

8. **To extend image coverage**, consider:
   - Visiting Frederiksborgmuseet (where many Rosenkrantz portraits are held) to request scans
   - Searching the Danish National Museum's portrait database (https://www.natmus.dk/)
   - Checking Rigsarkivet's probate records for any portrait inventories of deceased estates

---

## Source bibliography

- **Wikimedia Commons** — public-domain portraits (Holger Rosenkrantz, Jørgen Rosenkrantz, Niels Juel, Mogens Friis, Christianshavn 1751, Skovsbo)
- **Finn Holbek genealogy database** — Vibeke Podebusk portrait, Holger Axelsen Rosenkrantz
- **British Museum collection database** — Holger Axelsen Rosenkrantz sitter entry
- **National Portrait Gallery London** — Holger Rosenkrantz entry
- **RKD (Nederlands Instituut voor Kunstgeschiedenis)** — Jørgen Rosenkrantz painting
- **Rigsarkivet / Arkivalieronline** — parish records and standing registers
- **Weilbachs Kunstnerleksikon** — Hans Christopher Gedde biography
- **Dansk Biografisk Leksikon** — Christian Adam von Kleist, Niels Juel, Mogens Friis, Margrethe Ulfeldt, Christian Rosenkrantz
- **Wikipedia (da/en)** — most noble ancestors have biographical entries
- **WikiTree** — Krag-235 (Frederikke Louise Krag with full genealogy), Krag-51 (Niels Krag), Krag-246 (Niels Mogensen Krag), Rosenkrantz-78 (Jørgen Holgersen), Rosenkrantz-113 (Holger den Lærde)
- **Geni** — Niels Krag profile with estate inheritance info
- **Erik Brindt's genealogy** — Christian Rosenkrantz + Frederikke Louise Krag pages
- **sloev/tree GitHub repository** — family documents (Familien Gedde 1895, Vosnæsgaard 1839, Langø memoir, baptism and grave photos)
- **VKA Dubberow** — von Kleist family archive (https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf) for context and lineage table

---

*Catalog compiled November 2026. If you have additional family photographs or portraits to add, scan them and place them in `/home/z/my-project/upload/` for me to include in the next revision.*

# Archived file: leads/family-tree-evidence-audit.txt

==============================================================================
SLÆGTEN GÅRDSTED OG GEDDE — EVIDENCE AUDIT
Strengthening the weakest truths, node by node
==============================================================================
Date: 16 November 2026
Status: PROPOSED COMPILATION — not yet imported into canonical tree
Source tree: https://sloev.github.io/tree/ (489 canonical people)
Companion file: family-tree-expanded.txt (531 people after Nov 2026 expansion)

PURPOSE
=======
This file audits every weak-truth node in the expanded tree, starting with
the weakest. For each node, it lists:
  1. The current claim and its confidence level
  2. The sources currently cited
  3. ADDITIONAL corroborating sources found in this November 2026 pass
  4. The specific primary-source check that would settle the link

WEAKEST = (a) conf="possible" or "told", (b) no parents recorded,
         (c) sources restricted to user-sourced trees (WikiTree/Geni/MyHeritage),
         (d) note contains "sandsynligvis", "måske", "ikke fundet", "ifølge familien".

==============================================================================
PART 1 — VON KLEIST TYCHOW CHAIN (deepest past, strongest chain)
==============================================================================

After the 16 Nov 2026 correction (Reimer → Bartes mix-up fixed), the chain is:
  Hans Clest (I.24, c.1400)
    └─ Bartes II.3 (c.1425, d.1523)
       └─ Andreas II.11 (c.1500, efter 1546)
          └─ Carsten den Ældre II.21 (c.1535, før 1618)
             └─ Bernd II.39 (c.1565, efter 1665)
                └─ Christian II.60 (d.1679) — p6948
                   └─ Christian Casimir II.82 (1654-1722) — p3474

PRIMARY-SOURCE STRENGTH: STRONG but secondary.

Current evidence:
  • VKA Dubberow (English translation), p.117, 122-145
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf
  • H. W. Harbou, "Slægten von Kleist i Danmark",
    Personalhistorisk Tidsskrift 1897
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf

ADDITIONAL CORROBORATING SOURCES FOUND (Nov 2026):
  • L. Quandt, "Die Bearbeitung der Genealogie des Kleistschen Geschlechts"
    (genealogical treatise inside the VKA Geschichte, 1862-1887 edition)
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf
    — explicitly discusses "Der Lehnbrief von 1477" (the feudal letter of 1477)
    as the founding document for the Tychow-Dubberow line.
  • Albrecht Elzow, "Pommerscher Adelsspiegel" (c.1695) — article on Kleist
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/genealogie1.pdf
    — the earliest secondary source for the von Kleist genealogy.
  • Von der Osten, "Adelsspiegel" (1770) — extends Elzow
    https://dirkpeters.info/?page_id=283 (Pommerscher Adelsspiegel project)
    — names Bartes II.3's wife as "f. von Briesen" (confirms our correction).

PRIMARY-SOURCE CHECK NEEDED TO REACH "RECORD" CONFIDENCE:
  • The 1477 feudal letter itself (urk. 125 in VKA's urkundenbuch)
    — Landesarchiv Greifswald, Rep. 40 Nr. 35 gg, bl. 2-13 (per VKA archive
      overview https://www.v-kleist.com/bestand_greifswald.htm).
    — The original was sold to Peter Christian von Kleist in 1766; current
      archive text may be a copy. Inspect the Greifswald archive version.
  • Lenbrev 23/8 1540 (urk. 459k) — Andreas II.11's feudal letter.
  • Lenbrev 1/6 1546 (urk. 469) — Andreas II.11's re-investiture.
  • Lenbrev 22/2 1575 (urk. 502) — Carsten II.21's feudal letter.
  • Lenbrev 26/9 1618 (urk. 585) — Bernd II.39's feudal letter.
  • Skifte efter Christian von Kleist 1679 (Rendsborg skifteprotokol).
  Each of these would mention the parent's name explicitly.

NOT DONE: We have not yet inspected these primary documents. The chain
remains "record" in the sense that it is documented in a 19th-century
family-published genealogy citing medieval feudal letters — but the actual
letters have not been inspected in this research pass. Treat as
"probable → record" pending primary inspection.

==============================================================================
PART 2 — GEDDE OFFICER LINE ( bourgeoisie, not noble )
==============================================================================

NODE: Christian Giedde (p864's father) — conf="record" but birth year 1638 vs 1639 conflict.

Current claim in tree: Christian Giedde b.1638 Kville, d.1705 (buried Copenhagen)
  Source: Danish Wikipedia: https://da.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Giedde

CORRECTION FOUND (Nov 2026):
  Wikipedia explicitly states b.1639 in Kville, not 1638:
  "Christian Giedde, (født i Kville i 1639, blev begravet i København i 1705)."
  Bohuslän belonged to Sweden from 1658 — so "Kville" is in modern Sweden
  (Kville herred, Bohuslän), but in 1639 it was still Danish/Norwegian territory.

Christian Giedde's wife: Anna Margrethe Charlotte von Geijder
  Born 1648 on Øsel (Saaremaa, Estonia)
  Father: Carl Christian von Geijder, commander on Øsel (Courlandic nobility)
  Mother: Charlotte von Vietinghoff
  Source: https://da.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Giedde

ACTION: Update p864's note to reflect 1639 (Wikipedia explicit), not 1638.
Original baptism record for Christian Giedde in Kville kirkebog (pre-1814) at
Arkivalieronline. Kville sogn, Kville herred, Bohuslän — now in Sweden but
records were transferred; check Riksarkivet (Stockholm) for Swedish-era copy
or Arkivalieronline for the Danish-era scan.

==============================================================================
PART 3 — ROSENKRANTZ / KRAG / JUEL / ULFELDT NOBLE LINES
==============================================================================

The Rosenkrantz, Krag, Juel, Ulfeldt, Podebusk, Friis, Brahe, Gyldenstierne,
Høeg, and Sehested entries (the Danish noble lines around Christian
Rosenkrantz til Skovsbo and his wife Frederikke Louise Krag) currently rely
on WikiTree and Geni as primary cited sources. These are user-sourced
compilations. The CORROBORATING evidence we found in Nov 2026:

KEY FINDING: Almost every entry in these lines is documented in
**Danmarks Adels Aarbog (DAA)**, the official yearbook of Danish nobility
published since 1884 by the Foreningen til Udgivelse af Danmarks Adels Aarbog.
DAA is the gold-standard secondary source for Danish noble genealogy.

DAA accessible at:
  • https://www.familysearch.org/library/books/viewer/779881/ (1919 edition)
  • https://catalog.hathitrust.org/Record/011408261 (catalog record)
  • https://archive.org/details/danmarksadelaarb1900kj (selected volumes)
  • https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/samlinger/trap (Danish genealogy library)

For each noble person in our tree, the DAA cross-check is:

==============================================================================
PART 4 — GAARDSTED LINE — OPEN ANCHOR: CLAUS GAARDSTED'S PARENTS
==============================================================================

NODE: Claus Gaardsted (p72) b.ca.1750, d.1811 — forpagter Vosnæsgaard 1787-ca.1796,
      ejer Bogensholm 1796/97-1811.
  Currently: no parents recorded. note: "ukendt".

CURRENT SOURCES:
  • Skifteuddrag Rolsøgård G302 (1778-1816), p.39 (under Niels Jensen Degn
    probate no. 11, 14 May 1787): "Arv i boet til afdødes stedbørn efter
    Christen Christensen Tohde? foged i Dejret (skifte 1764) til datter
    Karen Christensdatter LV: broder Claus Gaardsted forpagter på Vosnæsgaard."
    Source: https://slaegt.dk/media/jnnnvuml/alfabetisk-navneliste-og-skifteuddrag-til-rols%C3%B8g%C3%A5rd-gods-g302-mols-herred-1778-1816-1818.pdf
  • Trap Danmark: Claus Gaardsted bought Bogensholm 1796 for 10,000 rigsdaler.
    https://trap5.lex.dk/sogn/vistoft-sogn-mols-herred/
  • Kongelig promemoria 6/8 1803: Claus as owner of Bogensholm.
    https://da.wikisource.org/wiki/Side:Kongelige_Rescripter...1803-1804).pdf/258

WHAT THE SOURCES SUPPORT:
  • Claus Gaardsted was Karen Christensdatter's brother.
  • Both were step-children of Christen Christensen Tohde, foged in Dejret.
  • The index points to an EARLIER 1764 probate for Tohde in the Rolsøgård
    estate records (before the indexed 1778-1816 volume).

WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SUPPORT:
  • Claus's biological parents are not named in the 1787 probate index.
  • The word "stedbørn" (step-children) means Tohde was their step-father,
    not their biological father. Their biological parent(s) are unknown
    from this index alone.

PRIMARY-SOURCE CHECK NEEDED:
  • The earlier Rolsøgård estate probate series for 1764 — search under
    Christen Christensen Tohde, possible spelling variants (Toft, Tofte),
    and Dejret parish. The Rolsøgård estate archive is at Landsarkivet
    for Sjælland (Rigsarkivet Viborg). The 1764 probate case would name:
    - Tohde's marital history
    - Names and relationships of his step-children
    - Identity of their biological parent(s)
    - Whether Claus Gaardsted is explicitly named as Karen's brother
  • Dejret kirkebog (Mols herred) for Claus's baptism around 1750.
    Pre-1814 ministerialbog at Arkivalieronline.
  • Vistoft sogn kirkebog for Claus's marriage to Kirsten Marie Jørgensdatter
    around 1780 — marriage entries typically name the bridegroom's father.

NEIGHBORING CONTEXT (not in tree):
  • Vosnæsgaard history (https://www.danskeherregaarde.dk/nutid/vosnaesgaard):
    The estate's owner history is documented; Claus was forpagter (tenant),
    not owner. Owner 1787 was likely the Dunker/Mørch family.
  • Bogensholm history at Trap Danmark: https://trap5.lex.dk/sogn/vistoft-sogn-mols-herred/
    The 1796 purchase for 10,000 rigsdaler is confirmed; Claus sold/was sold
    by widow in 1811. Auction records may survive.

ACTION: Schedule a reading-room visit at Rigsarkivet Viborg for the
Rolsøgård estate archive, specifically the 1764 probate series. Until
then, leave Claus's parents as "ukendt" with the existing note.

NODE: Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen (p6) b.1932-01-12 Egernsund, d.2009-05-06 Svendborg.

TWO COMPETING CLAIMS (preserved as separate relationship_claims in the tree):

CLAIM A — Legal paternity (recorded):
  Father: Wilhelm Krogh (p12), landarbejder Snogbæk
  Evidence:
  • Egernsund civil birth register 1932 nr. 4 (anmeldt 16/1 1932)
    — bsid 37658, billede 125 (Rigsarkivet AO).
  • Paternity declaration by Wilhelm Krogh, 5 April 1932.
  • Court decision, 10 June 1932.
  • Marginal note by registrar, 11 July 1932.

CLAIM B — Biological paternity (family account):
  Father: Sophus Petersen (sofus), b.1903-06-28 Nybøl, d.1945-08-24
  Evidence:
  • Family document: "Paula Mathilde Chrestle Gedde og Jens Jensens slægt"
    (typewritten family account).
  • Family handwritten anetavle.
  • Family accounts (oral tradition).
  • Note: "Familien identificerer gårdmandssønnen Sophus Petersen fra Påkjær
    som biologisk far; dette står i konflikt med den officielle registrering
    af Wilhelm Krogh."

ARCHIVE ROUTE FOR RESOLUTION (per leads/research-notes.md):
  Rigsarkivet guidance: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/
  Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt (effective 1 April 1932) archive:
  • Alimentationsjournal (and any register to it)
  • Almindelig Journal
  • Journalsager / Alimentationssager
  For 1920-1935, an unprinted finding aid is in the reading room at
  Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland, Aabenraa.
  Per archive overview (https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf,
  pp.91-92 / PDF lines 6228-6325).

ACTION:
  The civil register entry is already inspected and proves that Wilhelm
  declared paternity. The actual paternity case file (5 April declaration
  + 10 June court decision) has NOT been located in this pass.
  DNA testing would settle biological paternity.
  Until then: preserve both claims, do not commit to either.

==============================================================================
PART 13 — SUMMARY OF EVIDENCE STRENGTH
==============================================================================

Evidence strength categories used in this audit:

  STRONGEST (record, primary-source verified):
    - Direct kirkebog reading with LL: post citation: ~50 entries
    - Civil register entries (Lorenz Frederiksen's birth 1932): 1 entry
    - Direct AO image citations (Rosmus kirkebog 1789-1816): 3 entries

  STRONG (record, multi-source):
    - Wikipedia + Dansk Biografisk Leksikon + portrait painting:
      Holger Rosenkrantz, Jørgen Holgersen, Mogens Friis, Niels Juel,
      Holger Axelsen Rosenkrantz.
    - VKA Dubberow + Harbou 1897 + cited feudal letters (urk. 125, 502, 585):
      Hans Clest, Bartes II.3, Andreas II.11, Carsten II.21, Bernd II.39,
      Christian II.60, Christian Casimir II.82.

  MEDIUM (probable, single user-sourced):
    - WikiTree only entries in the Krag/Juel/Ulfeldt line — corroborated
      by Dansk Biografisk Leksikon entries for the famous members
      (Niels Juel, Margrethe Ulfeldt, Mogens Friis) but parents and
      great-grandparents are not always in DBL.
    - Cross-check against Danmarks Adels Aarbog (DAA) needed.

  WEAKEST (possible / told / unverified):
    - Anchors with no parents and only "Familiens håndskrevne anetavle"
      as source: ~15 entries (the older Langeland/Vestjylland branches).
    - WikiTree-only entries with no other corroborating source: 4 entries
      (some Langeland Christiansen family).
    - Open "fathers unknown" with no parish specified: ~10 entries.

  OPEN (cannot be verified from online sources alone):
    - Claus Gaardsted's parents: requires Rolsøgård probate 1764 visit.
    - Wilhelm Krogh's parents: requires Adelby kirkebog bog 2.3 request.
    - Lorenz biological father: requires DNA test.
    - Pre-1864 Sønderjylland records: some at Landesarchiv Schleswig.

==============================================================================
PART 14 — PRIORITISED NEXT-STEP ACTIONS
==============================================================================

A. ONE-TIME RIGSARKIVET VISITS (highest-yield):
   1. Rigsarkivet Viborg (Landsarkivet for Nørrejylland) — Mols herred
      skifteprotokoller for 1764: locate Tohde probate → Claus Gaardsted's
      parents.
   2. Rigsarkivet Aabenraa (Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland) — Aabenraa-
      Sønderborg Amt 1932 alimentationsjournal: Lorenz Frederiksen paternity.
   3. Arkivalieronline (online, free) — page-by-page reads of:
      - Fuglslev kirkebog pre-1814 (for Rasmus Jensen 1718, Jens Rasmussen 1755)
      - Viby (Roskilde amt) kirkebog pre-1814 (for Hans Jensen 1752 gartner)
      - Magleby (Langeland) kirkebog 1720-1830 (for Kirsten Andersdatter 1728,
        Christen Nielsen 1744, Thomas Jensen 1731, Niels Christiansen 1829,
        Inge Marie Rasmussen 1830, etc.)
      - Broager kirkebog 1800-1815 (for Frederiksen line anchors)
      - Fredensborg Slotssogn kirkebog 1778 (for Ove Samuel Gedde birth date)

B. ARCHION / GERMAN ARCHIVES:
   1. Adelby kirkebog bog 2.3 (1908) — request from Kirchenbuchamt
      Schleswig-Flensburg (contact: katja.isaack@kirche-slfl.de).
   2. Landesarchiv Greifswald Rep. 40 Nr. 35 gg — von Kleist feudal letters.

C. DNA TESTING:
   1. MyHeritage DNA test for one of Lorenz's daughters (already done per
      leads/investigation-worklist.md). 12,093 matches; 94 nearest reviewed.
   2. Cross-reference matches against Else-side and Sophus-side to isolate
      Sophus's contribution. DNA cousin-finder analysis.

D. BOOK / DAA RESEARCH:
   1. Danmarks Adels Aarbog (DAA) volumes covering Rosenkrantz, Krag, Juel,
      Ulfeldt, Friis, Podebusk, Brahe, Gyldenstierne, Sehested, Høeg.
      Available at: https://www.familysearch.org/library/books/viewer/779881/
      Look up each ★NY entry by name to confirm.
   2. C. E. A. Schøller, "Stamtavle over Officersfamilien Gedde (Gjedde)" —
      this would resolve the Christian Giedde 1638 vs 1639 question, the
      Fischer sisters' parents, and the Ove Samuel Gedde birth date.
   3. Lengnick's 1840 stamtavle (cited in leads/tree-research.md):
      https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/900782.pdf — for the von Kleist Tychow line
      (medieval generations, secondary source).

E. CODE / DATA ACTIONS:
   1. Update the build_data.py patch on p6948 (Christian von Kleist d.1679)
      to add father=vk_b39 and mother=vk_b39w (corrected lineage).
   2. Update p864 (Christian Giedde) birth year from 1638 to 1639 per
      Wikipedia explicit citation.
   3. Update p432 (Hans Christopher Gedde) birth date from "1738" to
      "1738-06-23" with bplace "Oldenburg" per Weilbach.
   4. Update rk_g_mf (Mogens Friis) birth date from 1623-04-16 to
      1623-09-07 per Wikipedia.
   5. Add kandidat B (Marthe Jørgensen b.9/10 1852) as g_kresten's mother
      with conf="probable".

♂ Claus Gaardsted (b.ca. 1750 d.1811) — død i Bogensholm, Vistoft (Mols) — Forpagter af Vosnæsgaard (1787–ca. 1796); Ejer af herregården Bogensholm (1796/97–1811) 📜
  📎 kilder: LL:0-414804; LL:1-475533; AO:Skødstrup kirkebog 1780–1809, s. 75; Trap Danmark / danskeherregaarde.dk (Bogensholm)
  📝 Gift to gange; Kirsten Marie Jørgensdatter var hans anden hustru. Enken solgte Bogensholm på auktion i 1811 for 31.000 rdl. Han kom til Vosnæsgaard efter 1784. Et skifteuddrag fra 1787 nævner hans søster Karen Christensdatter, som han var lavværge for. Hans fødested og forældre er stadig ukendte.

  📝 Nævnt som Claus Gaardsteds søster i et skifteuddrag fra 1787. Claus var hendes lavværge. Uddraget knytter hende til afdøde Christen Christensen Tohde? i Dejret, men angiver ikke forældrene med sikkerhed.

♂ Hans Clest (von Kleist) of Tychow (b.ca. 1400 d.før 1477) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Godsejer på Groß-Tychow 📜  ★NY
  📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.117 (omkring 1425, Tychow var sæde for Hans Clest I.24); Lenbrev 13/4 1477 (VKA urk. 125)
  📝 Den ældst kendte aner på von Kleist Tychow-linjen. Nævnes i 1425 som ejer af Tychow (VKA Dubberow p.117). Hans to sønner Reimer (II.2) og Bartes (II.3) nævnes i det fælles lenbrev af 13/4 1477 (urk. 125) som 'Brødre på Tychow'. Tessen (I.28) af Dubberow var hans nevø.
  ♂ Bartes von Kleist (b.ca. 1425 d.1523) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Godsejer på Groß-Tychow 📜  ★NY
    📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.122 (II.3 Bartes); Homage i Belgard 19/4 1477 (urk. 125); VKA Dubberow lineage table: 'Descendants of Bartes II.3' → 'Descendants of Bernd II.39' → 'Descendants of II.82'
    📝 Reimers bror (VKA Dubberow p.122). Deltog i homaget i Belgard 19/4 1477 (urk. 125). Siges at have levet til 1523 og være død af et fald fra hesten. Hans søn Andreas (II.11) er vor direkte aner — mens Reimers linje uddøde før 1672.
    ♂ Andreas (Drews) von Kleist (b.ca. 1500 d.efter 1546) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Godsejer på Groß-Tychow 📜  ★NY
      📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.122-123 (II.11 Andreas, son af II.3 Bartes); Lenbrev 23/8 1540 (urk. 459k); Lenbrev 1/6 1546 (urk. 469)
      📝 Bartes II.3's yngste søn (ikke Bartez II.6's søn — det var en tidligere fejli i denne fil). Nævnes som 'Drewes på Groten Tichow' og fik lenbrev 23/8 1540 (urk. 459k) og igen 1/6 1546 (urk. 469). I genealogien kaldes han oldefar til staldmester Joachim Henning (II.59, levede 1700). Hans hustrus navn er ikke nedskrevet. To brønde kendes: Martin II.9 og Jacob II.10.
      ♂ Carsten (den Ældre) von Kleist (b.ca. 1535 d.før 1618) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Officer i Wobser-korpset i Preussen 1556; Godsejer på Groß-Tychow og Mandelatz 📜  ★NY
        📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.122 (II.21 Carsten den Ældre, søn af II.11 Andreas); Lenbrev 22/2 1575 (urk. 502)
        📝 Kaldet 'den Ældre' for at skelne fra sin fætter Carsten den Yngre (II.19, søn af Joachim II.8 — altså fra Reimers linje, der uddøde). Tjente som officer i Preussen fra 1556, overtog senere de pommerske len på Tychow. Sammen med fætrene anlagde han i 1566 sag mod naboen Joachim Versen af Burzlaff. Fik fælles lenbrev 22/2 1575 (urk. 502). 8 sønner: Andreas, Joachim, Jacob, Henning, Ernst, Bernhard, …
        ♂ Bernd (Bernhard) von Kleist (b.ca. 1565 d.efter 1665) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Landkommissær og direktør for Belgard-kvartaler 1634-36; Godsejer på Groß-Tychow og Mandelatz 📜  ★NY
          📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.123-124 (II.39 Bernd, søn af II.21 Carsten); Lenbrev 26/9 1618 (urk. 585)
          📝 Carstens sjette søn. Efter farens død skulle han betale brødrene Henning og Tessen 1000 florin hver. Fik lenbrev 26/9 1618 (med broderen Jacob), igen 28/9 1621 og 5/9 1622. I 1629 blev han takseret for 5½ hide på Groß-Tychow. Tjenestegjorde som landkommissær 1634-36. Ved dokumentet af 1665 levede han endnu, men døde kort efter.
          ♂ Christian von Kleist (b.? d.1679) — Brandenborgsk oberst; Godsejer, Gross Tychow (Pommern) 📜
            📎 kilder: PHT:1897; VKA:Dubberow p.123-124 (II.39 Bernd)
            📝 Stamfar til den gren af slægten, der 'i henimod halvandet hundrede år blomstrede i Danmark'. Forældre tilføjet nov 2026 (rettet 16/11) baseret på VKA Dubberow p.123-124 (II.39 Bernd, søn af II.21 Carsten den Ældre, søn af II.11 Andreas, søn af II.3 Bartes, søn af I.24 Hans Clest af Tychow). Tidligere version af filen havde forkert parentage gennem Reimer II.2's linje (extinct før 1672) — rettet nu…

            ♂ Bernd Erdmann von Kleist (b.1651-11-25 d.1701-12-26) — død i Helsingør — Dansk kaptajn ved Schønfeldts Regiment 1676; Dansk major og oberstløjtnant ved Schacks Regiment 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.96; VKA:Dubberow p.144 (II.81)
              📝 Født på Groß-Tychow.blev som ung fængslet i Kolberg 1670 for 'ungdomsårsager'. Dansk kaptajn fra 1676, fangen ved Lund 1676, afskediget 1677. I dansk tjeneste igen fra 1684 ved Løwenschilds Regiment, fra nov 1684 ved Prinds Frederiks Regiment. Major 21/1 1688, oberstløjtnant 4/11 1688. Døde i Helsingør 26/12 1701, begravet i St. Mariæ Kirke.
            ♂ Adam Vilhelm von Kleist (b.ca. 1656 d.ca. 1690) — Kejserlig kaptajn ved Løwenschilds Dragonregiment 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.99; VKA:Dubberow p.144 (II.83)
              📝 Arvede Kiekow efter faderen og solgte det. I sin ungdom blev han lam i fødderne ved et ulykkestilfælde, men var 'så hurtig i ånden og så habil i forstanden', at han alligevel blev kejserlig kaptajn 1689. Gift med Cathrine Elisabeth von Kleist af Muttrin (datter af Pribislaff til Muttrin). Ingen sønner, men flere døtre.
            ♂ Sebastian (Bastian) Henrik von Kleist (b.ca. 1658 d.1714) — død i Mandalatz, Pommern — Kaptajnløjtnant ved Løwenschilds Regiment 1684; Premierløjtnant ved Fodgarden 1685; Kaptajn ved Schacks Regiment 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.99; VKA:Dubberow p.144-145 (II.84)
              📝 Fik ved arveskiftet halvparten af Groß-Tychow, som han byttede 1681 med Mandalatz, men købte den 1711 tilbage. Døde 1714 på Mandalatz. Gift med Lucie Juliane von Kleist (datter af Joachim Henning von Kleist af Groß-Tychow II.59 og Ilse Juliane von Creutz). Børn: Joachim Erdmann (c.1690-1745), Casimir Henning (1706-1755), Casimir (1709-ung), Carl Heinrich (1712), Sebastian Heinrich (1714-1774).
            ♀ Herath Juliane von Kleist (b.ca. 1660) — i Groß-Tychow, Pommern 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.99-100; VKA:Dubberow p.144 (II.85)
              📝 Ved faderens død enke efter Joachim Heinrich von Heydebreck af Schübben og Zuchen. 1682 forlovet med en 'Baron v. Borch', kurfyrstelig kammerretsråd i Berlin, senere gift med en Hr. v. Manteuffel. Begravet i Rendsborg 31/10 1802 (høj alder — muligvis en fejlskrivning for 1702).
            ♂ Bernd Christian von Kleist (b.ca. 1675) 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.100; VKA:Dubberow p.144 (II.85)
              📝 Søn af Christian von Kleists 2. ægteskab med Barbara Eleonora von Kalckstein. Arvede Oegeln og Kummeltitz efter moderen, men solgte Oegeln til stedfaderen Løwenschilds 2. hustru Marianne v. Plessen. Gift med ... v. Ritter fra Lausitz.
            ♀ [Datter von Kleist, gift i Schlesien] (b.ca. 1677) 📜  ★NY
              📎 kilder: PHT:1897 s.100; VKA:Dubberow p.144 (II.85)
              📝 Datter af Christian von Kleist og Barbara Eleonora von Kalckstein. Navn ikke nedskrevet i Harbou eller Dubberow. Gift i Schlesien.
  ♂ Reimer von Kleist [COLLATERAL — line extinct before 1672] (b.ca. 1425 d.efter 1477 (sandsynligvis til 1525)) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Rådsherre; hertugelig rådgiver for Bogislaw X af Pommern 📜  ★NY
    📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.117 (lineage table: 'Descendants of Reimar II.2, extinct before 1672'); VKA:Dubberow p.122 (II.2 Reimer)
    📝 BROR af Bartes II.3, ikke vores direkte aner. Nævnt i det fælles lenbrev af 13. april 1477 som 'Reymer' — bror til Bartes på Tychow. Optaget som nær rådgiver hos Hertug Bogislaw X sammen med kansler Nicolaus Damitz m.fl. Familien von Kleists overlevering siger, at han nåede en alder af omkring 100 år. Hans linje uddøde før 1672 ifølge VKA Dubberows lineage table (s.117) — derfor kan han IKKE være …
    ♂ Bartez (den Yngre) von Kleist [COLLATERAL — cousin of Andreas II.11] (b.ca. 1465 d.ca. 1527) — i Groß Tychow, Bagpommern — Godsejer på Groß-Tychow 📜  ★NY
      📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.122-123 (II.6 Bartez den Yngre, søn af II.2 Reimer); VKA Dubberow lineage table: 'Descendants of Reimar II.2, extinct before 1672'
      📝 Søn af Reimer II.2. Kaldet 'den Yngre' for at skelne fra sin onkel Bartes II.3 (vor direkte aner). Tidligere version af denne fil fejlagtigt anset ham for at være Andreas II.11's far — det er han IKKE. Hans linje uddøde før 1672.

♀ Hustru til Bartes von Kleist, f. von Briesen (b.ca. 1430) — i Pommern 📜  ★NY

♀ Hedwig von Heydebreck (b.ca. 1558) — i Schubben, Pommern ❓  ★NY
  📎 kilder: VKA:Dubberow p.122 (II.21 Carsten)
  📝 Carstens 2. hustru (gift ca. 1575). Ifølge von der Osten var hun hans niece: datter af Jacob von Heydebreck af Schubben og Sophia von Kleist (Carstens fætters søster). At gifte sig med en afdød broders datter var en almindelig praksis i den pommerske adel for at holde gods i familien.

      ♂ Jørgen Christensen (b.1829-03-09 d.1895) — i Skrøbelev — Smed og husmand på Nordenbrogård; Boede i Kurvemose 📜
        📎 kilder: LL:12-15556965; Familiens håndskrevne anetavle
        📝 Gift i Magleby ca. 1850 med Bodil Margrethe Clausdatter. De fik 9 børn; den ældste søn og den yngste datter blev i Danmark, resten udvandrede til Amerika. Jørgen og Bodil rejste også derover, men kom hjem igen efter nogle år.
        ∞ ♀ Bodil Margrethe Clausdatter (Clausen) (b.1829-03-28 d.1890) — i Magleby (Nordenbro), Langeland 📜

        ♀ Ane Marie Jørgensen (b.1859-09-07 d.1930) — i Skrøbelev, død i Langø, Lindelse Nor 📜
          📎 kilder: LL:12-15556964; LL:13-5085957; LL:9-1827831; LL:25-805445
          📝 Født i Kurvemose. Tjente på Baygården i Rudkøbing, der som den første på Langeland havde eget mejeri, og blev uddannet mejerske. Stod for økonomien på Langø. Den 7. sept., på hendes fødselsdag, gik hun som den første i land på Langeland ad den nye dæmning. Død på Langø 1930.
          ∞ ♂ Marius Michelsen Christensen (b.1858-04-14) — i Rudkøbing — Landbruger og ejer af øen Langø (1894–); Bygger af Langø-dæmningen (1911); Arbejdsmand ved Rudkøbing havn og Frihavnen i København 📜

A. GEDDE / VON KLEIST NOBLE LINE (Pommern → Denmark)
====================================================

Verified chain from Christian Casimir von Kleist (1654) back to Hans Clest (~1400):

  Hans Clest (von Kleist) of Tychow (ca.1400 — før 1477)   [VKA Dubberow p.117, I.24]
    ├─ Reimer II.2 (ca.1425 — efter 1477)  [COLLATERAL — line EXTINCT before 1672]
    │    ∞ f. von Born af Grassee                         [VKA Dubberow II.2]
    │    └─ Bartez (den Yngre) II.6 (ca.1465 — ca.1527)   [COLLATERAL]
    │         ∞ f. von Zozenow                            [VKA Dubberow II.6]
    │         (3 sønner II.7-8; line extinct before 1672)
    └─ Bartes II.3 (ca.1425 — 1523)  ← VOR DIREKTE ANER
         ∞ f. von Briesen                                  [VKA Dubberow p.122, II.3]
         └─ Andreas (Drews) von Kleist (ca.1500 — efter 1546)  [II.11]
              ∞ [hustru ukendt]
              └─ Carsten (den Ældre) von Kleist (ca.1535 — før 1618)  [II.21]
                   ∞ (1) [hustru] f. von Hechthausen
                   ∞ (2) Hedwig von Heydebreck (ca.1558)              [VKA II.21]
                   └─ Bernd (Bernhard) von Kleist (ca.1565 — efter 1665)  [II.39]
                        ∞ Elisabeth von Manteuffel af Pribbernow & Broitz [VKA II.39]
                        └─ Christian von Kleist (d.1679)  ← ALLEREDE p6948 i træet
                             ∞ (1) Hedvig Maria von Kleist  ← ALLEREDE p6949 i træet
                                  ∞ (2) Barbara Eleonora von Kalckstein  ★NY

CORRECTION (16 Nov 2026): En tidligere version af denne fil (15 Nov 2026)
fejlagtigt placerede Reimer II.2 og hans søn Bartez II.6 den Yngre i den
direkte linje, med Bartez II.6 som Andreas II.11's far. VKA Dubberow p.122
slår fast, at Reimer II.2 og Bartes II.3 var BRØDRE (begge sønner af Hans
I.24), og at Bartes II.3 (hvis hustru var f. von Briesen) — ikke Reimer —
er Andreas II.11's far. Reimers linje uddøde før 1672 (VKA Dubberow
lineage table p.117) og kan derfor IKKE være vor anelinje. Rettet nu.

Tidligere var Christian von Kleist (d.1679) træets dybeste kendte aner på
denne gren. Nu er 6 generationer tilføjet, så linjen nu når tilbage til
Hans Clest omkring 1400 (nævnt 1425) — en gevinst på ca. 280 år tilbage i tiden.

5 nye aner tilføjet (vk_h1 Hans, vk_b3 Bartes, vk_b11 Andreas, vk_b21 Carsten,
vk_b39 Bernd) + deres hustruer. Desuden er Reimer II.2 og Bartez II.6 den
Yngre medtaget som [COLLATERAL] for kontekst — de er vor anelinjes uddøde
sidegren. Desuden er Christians 5 børn fra 1. ægteskab + 2 børn fra 2.
Bernd Erdmann (1651-1701) dansk oberstløjtnant d. Helsingør, Adam Vilhelm,
Sebastian Henrik (d.1714), Herath Juliane, samt halvsøskende Bernd Christian og
en datter gift i Schlesien.

D. OPEN CONFLICTS — explicitly preserved (not silently resolved)
====================================================

• Ove Samuel Gedde birth date: 28 Feb 1778 (Harbou 1897, current tree)
  vs 28 May 1788 (Carl Johan Anker 1885, OCR unproofread). Original
  Fredensborg Slotssogn baptism register has NOT been inspected. The
  existing p216 entry retains 28 Feb 1778 with this conflict flagged.

• Hans Christopher Gedde's two wives: Frederikke Christiane Fischer
  (m.1, d.1787) and Øllegaard Sophie Fischer (m.2, 15 Feb 1789).
  Anker 1885 gives Ødegaard spelling; original marriage register
  should be checked before normalising.

• Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen paternity: civil register names Wilhelm
  Krogh (legal paternity, 5 Apr 1932 declaration + 10 Jun 1932 court
  decision). Family knowledge says biological father was Sophus Petersen.
  DNA needed to decide. Tree preserves both as separate relationship
  claims on p6; no change in this expansion.

• Two people share id p13 in some exports (Else Gedde Jensen and Lovisa
  Larsson). Do not merge by ID alone.

• Bornholm Lars Larsen (b.1771 Rutsker, d.1854 Allinge) was wrongly
  auto-linked to our Lars Larsen line. Explicitly REJECTED in
  data/auto_ancestors.json — must not be reintroduced.

E. NOTES ON OTHER LINES — work still open
====================================================

Several branches have anchor ancestors whose parents remain unidentified
in online sources. These were NOT filled with speculative entries; instead
they remain at their current depth with explicit gap markers. To extend
these lines, the following primary-source work is required (per
leads/investigation-worklist.md and leads/potential-leads-register.md):

  • Claus Gaardsted (ca.1750-1811, forpagter Vosnæsgaard, ejer Bogensholm)
    — Rolsøgård probate 1764 (Christen Christensen Tohde?) may identify his parents;
      original archive visit required. Mols herred skifteprotokoller.
  • Rasmus Jensen (ca.1718 Fuglslev) — Mols herred kirkebøger før 1814 læses
    direkte på Arkivalieronline (ikke indekseret).
  • Hans Jensen (ca.1752-1837 gartner Vibygård) — Viby kirkebog før 1814.
  • Kirsten Andersdatter (1728-1802 Langeland) — Magleby kirkebog før ~1750.
  • Christen Nielsen (1744-1804 Hennetved) — Magleby/Hennetved kirkebøger.
  • Jens Paulsen (ca.1799 Broager/Sønderborg) — tysk sognebog før 1864.
  • Peter Hansen (1803 Egernsund teglværksarbejder) — Broager kirkebog.
  • Sophus Petersens slægt (Paakjær, Nybøl, Dybbøl) — Nybøl/Dybbøl kirkebøger
    før 1864 ligger hos Landeskirchliches Archiv Schleswig.
  • Wilhelm Krogh (1908 Tastrup, Kreis Flensburg) — Adelby kirkebog bog 2.3
    hos Kirchenbuchamt Schleswig-Flensburg (katja.isaack@kirche-slfl.de).

Alle disse kræver manuel gennemlæsning af originale kirkebøger eller skifter —
de kan ikke løses alene via allerede tilgængelige online-kilder.

---

F. METHODOLOGICAL NOTE
====================================================

This expansion follows the editorial policy in leads/tree-research.md:
  • Only verified facts (with cited source) are entered as relationships.
  • Hypotheses, estimates, and unverified claims are NOT inserted as parent
    links. They remain research leads.
  • Medieval von Kleist material (Prissebur 1364, Klest de Densin 1289,
    Conradus/Bertoldus Clest 1248) is context-only and NOT added as direct
    ancestors — the generation-by-generation chain is not yet proven.
  • The von Kleist Tychow chain Hans I.24 → Bartes II.3 → Andreas II.11 →
    Carsten II.21 → Bernd II.39 → Christian II.60 IS proven by VKA Dubberow
    p.117, 122-145, which cites the feudal letters of 1477 (urk. 125),
    1503, 1540, 1546, 1575, 1585, 1594, 1601, 1605, 1608, 1618, 1621,
    1622, 1665. VKA Dubberow lineage table (p.117) explicitly labels
    'Descendants of Bartes II.3' as the line leading to Bernd II.39 → II.82
    (Christian Casimir), while Reimer II.2's line 'extinct before 1672'.

REVIEW HISTORY:
  • 15 Nov 2026 — first version of this file submitted; contained a real
    genealogy error: Reimer II.2 and Bartez II.6 the Younger were placed
    in the direct ancestor chain (with Bartez II.6 wrongly shown as
    Andreas II.11's father).
  • 16 Nov 2026 — error caught by external review (ChatGPT cross-check
    against the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow biographies). Confirmed against
    VKA Dubberow p.117, 122-123. Bartes II.3 (Reimer's brother) is the
    correct direct ancestor; Bartes II.3's wife was f. von Briesen, not
    f. von Zozenow. Reimer II.2's line is collateral (extinct before 1672).
    Hans Clest (I.24, mentioned c.1425) added as the earliest verified
    ancestor of the Tychow line. All corrections reflected in this revision.
